> COMMENT: Brennan's music used to be miscredited to Greg Holland, a graphic
>          artist at Melbourne House. Brennan's own comments are denoted (NB).
>          "The only musician I knew at Melbourne House pre-me (I think I started
>          in 1983) was Andrián Pertout. A guy named Gavin Anderson (I think) came
>          after me, but I lost contact with that company fairly quickly, so I'm
>          a pretty poor source of information." (NB)
>
> (#1)
> COMMENT: Sample taken from the film Enter the Dragon, in the scene where Lee
>          fights O'Hara with Lee knocking O'Hara to the ground over chairs.
> (#2)
>   TITLE: Dance of the Yao People
>  ARTIST: Liu Tieshan and Mao Yüan
> COMMENT: Composed in 1950, "Dance of the Yao People" combined folk melodies
>          and rhythms of the Yao people from the Yunan province, southwestern
>          China. Information comes from the liner notes for "Phases of the
>          Moon", a 1981 compact disk published by CBS Records.
>          "Fred Milgrom (founder of MH) gave me heaps of latitude, for which I
>          can only be grateful. I actually studied Chinese and Japanese
>          recordings and instruments for some of these pieces, absurdly serious
>          as that may sound." (NB)

| 1 | 10 | kernal-sensitive | 7.602e-2; 54754 | ΔRMS 4.586e-2; writes 54676; prefix 189; drift 304; derived | identical PCM; writes 54754; prefix 54754; drift 0; derived | ΔRMS 4.586e-2; writes 54676; prefix 189; drift 304 |

STIL (full available entry and applicable directory notes):

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/H/Hubbard_Rob/
> COMMENT: People have often stolen Hubbard's routine causing some tunes to be
>          falsely credited to him.
>          Hubbard's own comments are denoted (RH).
>
> COMMENT: "It is really, really, difficult to byte code the swing 16th feel that
>          is in the full version of this tune that I originally recorded. You
>          have to define long 16th and short 16th, and then when you get some
>          syncopated phrases it gets really messy.
>          So, I opted for a straight version. It refreshes faster than 50Hz to
>          speed the wobble chords up, and I added a bit of filter code." (RH)

> COMMENT: This is claimed to be the first ever C64 music made using samples.
>          According to Hülsbeck, though, the game "Bad Cat" was delayed about 3
>          months at Rainbow Arts during which time Imagine released their game
>          "Arkanoid", containing Martin Galway's sample-like technique.
>          "Arkanoid" thus became the first publically available game to feature
>          SID music with samples.
>          This is a cover from The Flirts's song Passion. The cover starts at
>          0:44 with the same bass. At 1:14 you will hear the melody from
>          Passion.

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/H/Huelsbeck_Chris/

>          More of Chris Hülsbeck's C64 music can be found in

>
> COMMENT: "The ingame music was supposed to sound a little like Reggae, because
>          the Rainbow Arts Boss was a fan of Bob Marley &amp; others and the Title
>          music [...] was a homage to True Blue from Madonna, also one of his
>          favorites. In the end I think, despite the inspirations, the songs
>          have their own character and are the only part of the game that wasn't
>          heavily borrowed from Super Mario Brothers, which is something I am
>          proud of." (CH)
